\displaystyle\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{t}\leq C_{\Lambda}\exp(C_{\Lambda}C_{\tilde{b}}t).

(134)

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

A.4
Proof of Proposition
4.25

Lemma A.2
.

a.s.
\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}\to\tilde{Z}^{x}
in
\mathbb{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d})
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.

Let

\tilde{X}^{y^{(n)}}(t)=y^{(n)}+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s))\,ds+\sigma B(t),\qquad n\in\mathbb{N},

and let

\tilde{X}^{x}(t)=x+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\,ds+\sigma B(t).

Then,

\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}=\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{y^{(n)}}),\quad\text{ and }\quad\tilde{Z}^{x}=\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x}).

By Lipschitz continuity of the Skorokhod map
\Gamma
and Assumption
4.12
, for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}-\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{t}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Gamma}\|\tilde{X}^{y^{(n)}}-\tilde{X}^{x}\|_{t}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Gamma}\left(\|y^{(n)}-x\|_{\infty}+C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}-\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{s}\,ds\right).

By Grönwall’s inequality,

\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}-\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{t}\leq C_{\Gamma}\|y^{(n)}-x\|_{\infty}\cdot\exp(C_{\Gamma}C_{\tilde{b}}t).

Thus, as
n\to\infty
,
\|y^{(n)}-x\|_{\infty}\to 0
, and

\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}-\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{t}\to 0\quad\text{for every }t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.

This completes the proof of the lemma. ∎

Proof of Proposition
4.25
.

Let
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,d\}
.
For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define

\Psi^{y^{(n)}}(t):=e_{j}+\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s))D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)\,ds,

(135)

and define

\Psi^{x}(t):=e_{j}+\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\,ds,

(136)

where
e_{j}
is the
j
th standard unit vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then a.s.
D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi^{x}]
, and for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}[\Psi^{y^{(n)}}]
.

Let
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Then

\displaystyle\|\Psi^{y^{(n)}}(t)-\Psi^{x}(t)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s))D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)\,ds-\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\,ds\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}\left\|D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s))\left(D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\right)\right\|_{\infty}\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\left(D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s))-D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\right)D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\right\|_{\infty}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}\,\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}ds.

Here, the preceding inequality follows from Assumption
4.12
.

We now examine the terms

C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}\,ds\quad\text{and}\quad C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}\,\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}ds,

respectively. First consider the latter term. Because by Lemma
4.24
,
\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{t}<\infty
,
and by Proposition
4.16
,

\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}-\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{t}<C\|y^{(n)}-x\|_{\infty}
for some
C
that does not depend on
n
or
x
,

C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}\,\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}ds\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Now consider

C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}\,ds.

Recall that a.s.
D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{y^{(n)}})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}

and
D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})
. Thus, a.s.,

\displaystyle\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{y^{(n)}})(s)-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{y^{(n)}})(s)-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\|_{\infty}+\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})(s)-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{y^{(n)}})-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})\|_{s}+\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})(s)-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Lambda}\|\Psi^{y^{(n)}}-\Psi^{x}\|_{s}+\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})(s)-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\|_{\infty}.

Thus,

\int_{0}^{t}\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}ds\leq C_{\Lambda}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi^{y^{(n)}}-\Psi^{x}\|_{s}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})(s)-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\|_{\infty}ds.

By Proposition
4.23
,
\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})\to\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})
in
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Furthermore, both
\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})\|_{t}
and
\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})\|_{t}
are uniformly bounded, so by the fact that

\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\to\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\text{ for }s\in[0,t]\text{ a.e.,}

and dominated convergence theorem,

\int_{0}^{t}\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})(s)-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\|_{\infty}ds\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

To summarize, we have established that

\|\Psi^{y^{(n)}}(t)-\Psi^{x}(t)\|_{\infty}\leq C_{n}(t)+C_{\tilde{b}}C_{\Lambda}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi^{y^{(n)}}-\Psi^{x}\|_{s}ds,

where

C_{n}(t)=C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}}(\Psi^{x})(s)-\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(\Psi^{x})(s)\|_{\infty}ds+C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}\,\|\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\|_{\infty}^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}ds.

Since
C_{n}(\cdot)
is non-decreasing for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we also have

\|\Psi^{y^{(n)}}-\Psi^{x}\|_{t}\leq C_{n}(t)+C_{\tilde{b}}C_{\Lambda}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi^{y^{(n)}}-\Psi^{x}\|_{s}ds.

By Grönwall’s inequality, and the fact that
C_{n}(t)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for each
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we have

\|\Psi^{y^{(n)}}-\Psi^{x}\|_{t}\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Because
x\in\mathbb{R}_{++}^{d}
,
\Psi^{x}(0)=e_{j}\in H_{x}
. Thus, by Proposition
4.23
, a.s.

D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}\to D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{ as }n\to\infty.

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

A.5
Proof of Corollary
4.27

By the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\|I(y^{(n)})(s)-I(x)(s)\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq 4\,\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\|\sigma^{-1}(D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}(s)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\|_{2}^{2}ds\right]\to 0.

as
n\to\infty
. The convergence follows from dominated convergence theorem, and the fact that
D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}\to D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}
in
\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
and both
\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{y^{(n)}}\|_{t}
and
\|D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{t}
are uniformly bounded.
This completes the proof of the corollary.

Appendix B
Proof of Theorem
6.8

We prove Theorem
6.8
in this section.
For a random variable
X
, write
\|X\|:=(\mathbb{E}[X^{2}])^{1/2}
. For
i\in\{1,\dots,d\}
and any
\theta\in\Theta
, denote
v^{\theta,i}_{n,M}(t,x):=[v^{\theta}_{n,M}(t,x)]_{i}
.
We show
v^{0}_{n,M}(t,x)
is close to
\tilde{v}(t,x)
; the bound for
V^{0}_{n,M}(t,x)
will follow similarly.
By the triangle inequality, for each
i
,

\left\|v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)-\tilde{v}_{i}(t,x)\right\|\leq\left\|v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)-\mathbb{E}\left[v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right]\right\|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right]-\tilde{v}_{i}(t,x)\right|.

(137)

Because for different
\theta\in\Theta
,
\left(S^{\theta},\tilde{Z}^{t,x}_{\theta}(S^{\theta}),\tilde{Z}^{t,x}_{\theta}(T),\mathcal{V}^{\theta}(S^{\theta};t,x),\mathcal{P}^{\theta}(T;t,x),\mathcal{V}^{\theta}(T;t,x)\right)

are i.i.d. copies of the tuple

\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(S),\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T),\mathcal{P}(T;t,x),\mathcal{V}(S;t,x),\mathcal{V}(T;t,x)\right),

we have

\displaystyle~Var\left(v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M^{n}}\cdot Var\Bigg(C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)+\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)+\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}\frac{C^{2}(T-t)}{M^{n-\ell}}\cdot Var\Bigg(\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\Bigg[\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v^{(0,\ell,1)}_{\ell,M}\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v^{(0,-\ell,1)}_{\ell-1,M}\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)\Bigg]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M^{n}}\cdot\left\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)+\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)+\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}\frac{C^{2}(T-t)}{M^{n-\ell}}\cdot\Bigg\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\Bigg[\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v^{(0,\ell,1)}_{\ell,M}\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v^{(0,-\ell,1)}_{\ell-1,M}\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)\Bigg]\Bigg\|^{2}.

In the preceding inequality, we used the simple fact that
\mathrm{Var}(X)\leq\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]=\|X\|^{2}
. Applying the triangle inequality again and noting that

Var\left(v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right)=\left\|v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)-\mathbb{E}\left[v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right]\right\|^{2},

(138)

we have

\displaystyle~\left\|v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)-\mathbb{E}\left[v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right]\right\|

(139)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M^{n/2}}\cdot\left\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)+\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)+\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|

(140)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}\frac{C(T-t)}{M^{(n-\ell)/2}}\Bigg\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\Bigg[\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v^{(0,\ell,1)}_{\ell,M}\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)

(141)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v^{(0,-\ell,1)}_{\ell-1,M}\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)\Bigg]\Bigg\|

(142)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M^{n/2}}\cdot\left\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)+\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)+\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|

(143)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}\frac{C(T-t)}{M^{(n-\ell)/2}}\Bigg\|\left|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\right|\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}C_{i_{1}}\Bigg|\left(v^{(0,\ell,1),i_{1}}_{\ell,M}-v^{(0,-\ell,1),i_{1}}_{\ell-1,M}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\Bigg|\Bigg\|

(144)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M^{n/2}}\cdot\left\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)+\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)+\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|

(145)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}\frac{C(T-t)}{M^{(n-\ell)/2}}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}C_{i_{1}}\Bigg\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{\ell,M}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\Bigg\|

(146)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}\frac{C(T-t)}{M^{(n-\ell)/2}}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}C_{i_{1}}\Bigg\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{\ell-1,M}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\Bigg\|.

(147)

We now wish to bound
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right]-\tilde{v}_{i}(t,x)\right|
.
To do so, we establish the following lemma.

Lemma B.1
.

We have, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
i=1,2,\cdots,d
,

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right]

(148)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)+\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right]

(149)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[C(T-t)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v^{0}_{n-1,M}\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)\right].

(150)

We skip the proof of the lemma, which is standard and similar to, e.g., that of Lemma 3.3 (iii) in
[
65
]
.
The main fact used is that
v^{\theta}_{\ell,M}
are i.i.d for different
\theta\in\Theta
.

Using the fact the
\tilde{v}
is a fixed point of
F
, we also have

\displaystyle~\left|\mathbb{E}\left[v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)\right]-\tilde{v}_{i}(t,x)\right|

(151)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~C(T-t)\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v^{0}_{n-1,M}\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)-\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right),v\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right)\right)\right]\right|

(152)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(T-t)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}C_{i_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{n-1,M}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right|\right]

(153)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~C(T-t)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}C_{i_{1}}\left\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{n-1,M}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right\|.

(154)

From expressions (
145
) – (
147
) and (
154
), it follows that

\displaystyle~\left\|v^{0,i}_{n,M}(t,x)-\tilde{v}_{i}(t,x)\right\|

(155)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M^{n/2}}\cdot\left\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)+\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)+\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|

(156)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}\frac{C(T-t)}{M^{(n-\ell)/2}}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}C_{i_{1}}\Bigg\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{\ell,M}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\Bigg\|

(157)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}\frac{C(T-t)}{M^{(n-\ell)/2}}\cdot\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}C_{i_{1}}\Bigg\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{\ell-1,M}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\Bigg\|

(158)

\displaystyle~+C(T-t)\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}C_{i_{1}}\left\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{n-1,M}-\tilde{v}_{i}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\right\|

(159)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M^{n/2}}\cdot\left\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)+\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)+\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|

(160)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}\frac{2C(T-t)C_{i_{1}}}{M^{(n-\ell-1)/2}}\cdot\Bigg\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{\ell,M}-\tilde{v}_{i}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\Bigg\|

(161)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M^{n/2}}\left[\left\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\right\|+\left\|\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)\right\|+\left\|\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|\right]

(162)

\displaystyle~+\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}\frac{2C(T-t)C_{i_{1}}}{M^{(n-\ell-1)/2}}\cdot\Bigg\|\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(S;t,x\right)\cdot\left(v^{0,i_{1}}_{\ell,M}-\tilde{v}_{i}\right)\left(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\left(S\right)\right)\Bigg\|.

(163)

We now upper bound terms in the preceding expression respectively.
First, we have

\displaystyle\quad\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(S))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}(S;t,x)\|

\displaystyle=C(T-t)\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{c}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(S))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(S;t,x)\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=C(T-t)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{c}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(S;t,x)\right]\Big|S=s\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=C(T-t)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\frac{e^{-\beta(s-t)}}{\sqrt{s-t}}\cdot\frac{1}{C(T-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{c}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(s;t,x)\right]ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\underline{c}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(s;t,x)\right]ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}[\underline{c}^{4}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{V}_{i}^{4}(s;t,x)]^{1/2}ds\right)^{1/2}.

By Corollary
4.17
,

\mathbb{E}[\underline{c}^{4}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))]^{1/2}\leq\alpha_{w}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[w^{4}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)\right)\right]^{1/2}\leq\alpha_{w}^{2}C_{4,T}^{1/2}w^{2}(x).

(164)

By Assumption
6.5
, and the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{V}_{i}^{4}(s;t,x)]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{s-t}}\int_{t}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]^{\top}_{i}dB(r)\right)^{4}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{4}}{s-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{t}^{s}\|\sigma^{-1}D_{i}\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\|^{2}dr\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{4}C_{\sigma,D},

for some universal constant
C_{4}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\quad\|C(T-t)\cdot\underline{c}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(S))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}(S;t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}[\underline{c}^{4}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{V}_{i}^{4}(s;t,x)]^{1/2}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}\alpha_{w}^{2}C_{4,T}^{1/2}C_{4}C_{\sigma,D}w^{2}(x)\right)^{1/2}\leq\underline{C}w(x),

for some positive constant
\underline{C}
that is independent of
d
.

For
\|\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)\|
, we claim that
\|\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)\|\leq C_{\cal P}w(x)
for some
constant
C_{\cal P}
that is independent of
d
and
i
.
By product rule, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\beta(s-t)}\kappa^{\top}d\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\beta(T-t)}\kappa^{\top}\tilde{Y}^{x}(T)-\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)de^{-\beta(s-t)}\right].

Thus,

\displaystyle D_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\beta(s-t)}\kappa^{\top}d\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)\right]=

\displaystyle~D_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\beta(T-t)}\kappa^{\top}\tilde{Y}^{x}(T)-\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)de^{-\beta(s-t)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\beta(T-t)}\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{x}(T)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)de^{-\beta(s-t)}\right],

so

\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\beta(T-t)}\kappa^{\top}D_{i}\tilde{Y}^{x}(T)-\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}D_{i}\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)de^{-\beta(s-t)}\right].

By Lemma
4.24
, it follows that
\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\left\|D_{i}\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)\right\|_{\infty}
is upper bounded by a constant that is independent of
d

and
i
. By Assumption
6.7
, it follows that
\left|\kappa^{\top}D_{i}\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)\right|/\left(1+(\log d)^{\alpha_{0}/2}\right)
is upper bounded by a constant that is independent of
d
,
i
,
and
s
. Therefore,

\left|\mathcal{P}_{i}(T;t,x)\right|\leq C_{\cal P}w(x),

for some constant
C_{\cal P}
that is independent of
d
and
i
.

For the term
\left\|\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{e^{-\beta(T-t)}}{\sqrt{T-t}}\xi\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T)\right)\mathcal{V}_{i}\left(T;t,x\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|(T-t)^{-1/2}\xi(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\mathcal{V}_{i}(T;t,x)\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(T-t)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(T;t,x)\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(T-t)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{4}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\right]^{1/4}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{V}_{i}^{4}(T;t,x)\right]^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(T-t)^{-1/2}\alpha_{w}C_{4,T}^{1/4}w(x)\cdot\sqrt{C_{4}C_{\sigma,D}}.

Next,

\displaystyle\quad\|C(T-t)(v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}})(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(S))\mathcal{V}_{i}(S;t,x)\|

\displaystyle=C(T-t)\left(\int_{t}^{T}\frac{e^{-\beta(s-t)}}{\sqrt{s-t}}\cdot\frac{1}{C(T-t)}\mathbb{E}\left[(v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i})^{2}(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(s;t,x)\right]ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[(T-s)\cdot(v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i})^{2}(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(s;t,x)]ds\right)^{1/2}

Define, for a random function
v:[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
,

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}}\frac{(T-s)^{1/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[v^{2}(s,x)]^{1/2}}{w(x)},\quad s\in[0,T).

Then, we continue with the bounds above to get

\displaystyle\quad\|C(T-t)(v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}})(S,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(S))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}(S;t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[(T-s)\cdot(v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i})^{2}(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(s;t,x)]ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\Bigg(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[w^{2}(z)\cdot\frac{(T-s)(v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}})^{2}(s,z)y_{i}^{2}}{w^{2}(z)}\Bigg|\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)=z,\mathcal{V}(s;t,x)=y\right]\right]ds\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{C(T-t)}\Bigg(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[w^{2}(z)y_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(T-s)(v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}})^{2}(s,z)}{w^{2}(z)}\right]\Bigg|\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)=z,\mathcal{V}(s;t,x)=y\right]ds\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}[w^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\cdot\mathcal{V}_{i}^{2}(s;t,x)]\cdot{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}[w^{4}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{V}_{i}^{4}(s;t,x)]^{1/2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C(T-t)}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}C_{4,T}^{1/2}w^{2}(x)C_{4}C_{\sigma,D}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}w(x)\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}

for some positive constant
\tilde{C}
that is independent of
d
.

In summary, we have established that

\displaystyle\quad\|v_{n,M}^{0,i}(t,x)-\tilde{v}_{i}(t,x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{M^{n/2}}\left(\frac{\tilde{C}_{0}}{\sqrt{T-t}}w(x)+C_{\cal P}w(x)\right)+

\displaystyle\qquad\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}\frac{2C_{i_{1}}\tilde{C}w(x)}{M^{(n-\ell-1)/2}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{C}_{1}w(x)}{M^{n/2}\sqrt{T-t}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}\frac{2\tilde{C}C_{i_{1}}w(x)}{M^{(n-\ell-1)/2}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}\frac{1}{(s-t)^{3/4}}\frac{1}{(T-s)^{3/4}}ds\right)^{1/3}\left(\int_{t}^{T}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0,i_{1}}-\tilde{v}_{i_{1}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{6}ds\right)^{1/6},

where the last inequality follows from Hölder’s inequality. Thus,

\displaystyle\quad\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(v_{n,M}^{0,i}(t,x)-\tilde{v}_{i}(t,x)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{C}}{M^{n/2}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}\frac{2\bar{C}C_{i_{1}}}{M^{(n-\ell-1)/2}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0}-\tilde{v}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{6}ds\right)^{1/6},

(165)

where, in the last inequality, with a slight abuse of notation, we defined

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}:=\max_{j=1,\dots,J}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}}\frac{(T-s)^{1/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[v_{j}^{2}(s,x)]^{1/2}}{w(x)}=\max_{j=1,\dots,J}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{j}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s},

for random vector-valued function
v:[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
, used the fact that

\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-3/4}(T-s)^{-3/4}ds<\infty,

and defined the constant
\bar{C}
by

\bar{C}:=\max\left\{\tilde{C}_{1},\tilde{C}\sqrt{T-t}\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-3/4}(T-s)^{-3/4}ds\right)^{1/3}\right\}.

Because the right-hand side of Ineq. (
165
) is independent of
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
and
i=1,\cdots,d
,
we can conclude that

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{n,M}^{0}-\tilde{v}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{C}}{M^{n/2}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{d}\frac{2\bar{C}C_{i_{1}}}{M^{(n-\ell-1)/2}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0}-\tilde{v}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{6}ds\right)^{1/6}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\bar{C}}{M^{n/2}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\frac{2\bar{C}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}}{M^{(n-\ell-1)/2}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0}-\tilde{v}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{6}ds\right)^{1/6}.

By Lemma 3.11 in
[
66
]
, we have

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{n,M}^{0}-\tilde{v}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\bar{C}+2\bar{C}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}(T-t)^{1/6}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\tilde{v}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}\right)\times

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\frac{M^{3}}{6}\right)M^{-n/2}\left(1+2\bar{C}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}(T-t)^{1/6}\right)^{n-1}.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\bar{C}+2\bar{C}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}(T-t)^{1/6}\|\tilde{v}\|_{0}\right)\times

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\frac{M^{3}}{6}\right)M^{-n/2}\left(1+2\bar{C}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}(T-t)^{1/6}\right)^{n-1},

where we recall that the norm
\|\cdot\|_{0}
was defined in Eq. (
43
) with
\rho=0
.

By similar reasoning, we can also show that

\frac{\|V_{n,M}(t,x)-\tilde{V}(t,x)\|}{w(x)}\leq\frac{\bar{C}_{V}}{M^{n/2}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\frac{2\bar{C}_{V}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}}{M^{(n-\ell-1)/2}}\left(\int_{t}^{T}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|v_{\ell,M}^{0}-\tilde{v}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}^{6}ds\right)^{1/6},

for some positive constant
\bar{C}_{V}
that is independent of
d
. This implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}}\frac{\|V_{n,M}(t,x)-\tilde{V}(t,x)\|}{w(x)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\bar{C}_{V}+2\bar{C}_{V}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}(T-t)^{1/6}\|\tilde{v}\|_{0}\right)\times

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\frac{M^{3}}{6}\right)M^{-n/2}\left(1+2\bar{C}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}(T-t)^{1/6}\right)^{n-1}.

Finally, with a slight abuse of notation, define

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(V,v)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[V(s,x)^{2}]^{1/2}}{w(x)}\vee\max_{j=1,\dots,J}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}}\frac{(T-s)^{1/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[v_{j}^{2}(s,x)^{2}]^{1/2}}{w(x)}.

(166)

Then, by appropriately redefining
\bar{C}
, we have

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(V_{n,M}^{0},v_{n,M}^{0}\right)-\left(\tilde{V},\tilde{v}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\bar{C}+2\bar{C}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}(T-t)^{1/6}\|\tilde{v}\|_{0}\right)\times

\displaystyle\times\exp\left(\frac{M^{3}}{6}\right)M^{-n/2}\left(1+2\bar{C}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}(T-t)^{1/6}\right)^{n-1}.

We now follow a similar reasoning as the proof of Corollary 5.1 in
[
65
]
to derive computational complexity bounds.
To simplify notation, define a new constant
\bar{C}_{T}
by

\bar{C}_{T}:=1+\bar{C}+2\bar{C}(T-t)^{1/6}.

Then,

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(V_{n,M}^{0},v_{n,M}^{0}\right)-\left(\tilde{V},\tilde{v}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\max\{1,\|\tilde{v}\|_{0}\}\exp\left(\frac{M^{3}}{6}\right)M^{-n/2}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{n-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{M^{3}}{6}\right)M^{-n/2}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{n}\max\{1,\|\tilde{v}\|_{0}\}.

Therefore
4
4

4

For the remainder of the proof, we will treat parameters such as
n^{1/3}
and
N^{1/3}
as if they were guaranteed to be integers.
Rounding them up or down to a nearest integer would overburden the notation, but would have no effect on our order-of-magnitude estimates.
,

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(V_{n,n^{1/3}}^{0},v_{n,n^{1/3}}^{0}\right)-\left(\tilde{V},\tilde{v}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{n/6}n^{-n/6}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{n}\max\{1,\|\tilde{v}\|_{0}\}.

Complexity analysis.

Let
RS
denote the expected runtime of the exact simulation algorithm with polynomial complexity for the tuple (
66
),
and let
RO
denote the runtime of the polynomial-complexity optimization algorithm for solving
\tilde{\cal H}
.
Let
\underline{RT}=RS+2\cdot RO
be a “base” runtime.
Define
RT(n,M)
as the expected runtime needed to produce one realization of
\big(V^{0}_{n,M}(t,x),v^{0}_{n,M}(t,x)\big)
. The multilevel construction (
67
)–(
74
) yields the recurrence

RT(n,M)\;\leq\;\underline{RT}\cdot M^{n}\,+\,\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}M^{\,n-\ell}\,\Big(\underline{RT}\,+\,RT(\ell,M)\,+\,RT(\ell{-}1,M)\Big),\quad n\geq 1,

(167)

with
RT(0,M)=0
. Using the same reasoning as the proof of Lemma 3.6 in
[
67
]
, we can establish that

RT(n,M)\;\leq\;5\cdot\underline{RT}\cdot(2M{+}1)^{n}\;\leq\;5\cdot\underline{RT}\cdot(3M)^{n},\qquad n\in\mathbb{N}.

(168)

For notational convenience, let

\varepsilon(n,M):=\frac{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\left(V_{n,M}^{0},v_{n,M}^{0}\right)-\left(\tilde{V},\tilde{v}\right)\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{t}}{\max\{1,\|\tilde{v}\|_{0}\}},

(169)

where we recall the definition of
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{s}
in Eq. (
80
).
Since

\varepsilon\left(n,n^{1/3}\right)\leq e^{n/6}n^{-n/6}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{n},

and
e^{n/6}n^{-n/6}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, we have
\varepsilon\left(n,n^{1/3}\right)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.
Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
, and define

N_{\varepsilon}:=\min\left\{n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}:\varepsilon\left(n,n^{1/3}\right)\leq\varepsilon\text{ for all }n>n_{0}\right\}.

(170)

Write
N=N_{\varepsilon}
for notational simplicity. Then we claim that

\varepsilon\leq e^{N/6}N^{-N/6}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{N}.

(171)

Indeed, if
N=1
, then
e^{N/6}N^{-N/6}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{N}=1>1/2>\varepsilon
;
if
N\geq 2
, then by minimality of
N
, we must have

\varepsilon<\varepsilon\left(N,N^{1/3}\right)\leq e^{N/6}N^{-N/6}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{N}.

Thus, we have established the claim. Therefore,

\displaystyle RT\left((N+1),(N+1)^{1/3}\right)\leq

\displaystyle~5\cdot\underline{RT}\cdot\left(3(N+1)\right)^{(N+1)/3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~5\cdot\underline{RT}\cdot\left(3(N+1)\right)^{(N+1)/3}\cdot\varepsilon^{-4}\left(e^{N/6}N^{-N/6}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{N}\right)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~5\cdot\underline{RT}\cdot\varepsilon^{-4}\left[\left(3(N+1)\right)^{(N+1)/3}e^{2N/3}N^{-2N/3}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{4N}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~5\cdot\underline{RT}\cdot\varepsilon^{-4}\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left[\left(3(N+1)\right)^{(N+1)/3}e^{2N/3}N^{-2N/3}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{4N}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~15\alpha_{1}(1+d^{\alpha_{2}})\cdot\varepsilon^{-4}\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left[\left(3(N+1)\right)^{(N+1)/3}e^{2N/3}N^{-2N/3}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{4N}\right],

where
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are from Definitions
6.1
and
6.2
.
Under Assumption
6.3
, the quantity

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\left[\left(3(N+1)\right)^{(N+1)/3}e^{2N/3}N^{-2N/3}\left(\bar{C}_{T}C_{\tilde{\cal H}}\right)^{4N}\right]

(172)

is a constant independent of
d
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
6.8
.

Appendix C
Proofs for Section
8

C.1
Proof of Proposition
8.2

To prove Proposition
8.2
, we introduce some notation and establish a useful lemma.
For
\varepsilon>0
, define the process
\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)
by

\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(t):=\frac{\tilde{Z}^{x}(t;\varepsilon\Upsilon)-\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)}{\varepsilon},\quad t\geq 0,

(173)

and define the process
{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}
by

{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s;\varepsilon\Upsilon))-\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))}{\varepsilon}\,ds+\Upsilon(t),\quad t\geq 0.

(174)

Let

\tilde{X}^{x}(t;\varepsilon\Upsilon):=x+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s;\varepsilon\Upsilon))\,ds+\varepsilon\Upsilon(t)+\sigma B(t),\qquad t\geq 0.

(175)

Then, for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\tilde{X}^{x}(t;\varepsilon\Upsilon)

\displaystyle=x+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\,ds+\sigma B(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\left[\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s;\varepsilon\Upsilon))-\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\right]\,ds+\varepsilon\Upsilon(t)

\displaystyle=\tilde{X}^{x}(t)+\varepsilon{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}(t).

Thus, by definition,

\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\frac{\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x}+\varepsilon{\Psi}^{\varepsilon})-\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})}{\varepsilon}=\nabla_{{\Psi}^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x}).

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose Assumptions
4.12
and
3.3
hold.
Let
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
, and suppose a.s.
\nabla_{\psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
exists for all
\psi\in\mathbb{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
and lies in
\mathbb{D}_{\ell,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Let
\Upsilon(\cdot)
and
\upsilon(\cdot)
be the same as in Proposition
8.2
.
Then, there exists a unique
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}
-adapted process
\Phi
such that a.s.
\Phi\in\mathbb{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies

\Phi=\nabla_{{\Psi}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x}),

where
{\Psi}
is a
J
-dimensional continuous
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}
-adapted process with

{\Psi}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\Phi(s)\,ds+\Upsilon(t),\quad t\geq 0.

Furthermore, for any
t\geq 0
,

\|{\Psi}\|_{t}<C_{\upsilon}t\cdot\exp\left(C_{\tilde{b}}C_{\Gamma}t\right),\ \ \text{and}\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\|\Phi^{\varepsilon}-\Phi\|_{t}=0,

where we recall the definition of
\Phi^{\varepsilon}
in Eq. (
174
).

Proof.

We first show the uniqueness of the process
\Phi
.
Suppose both
\Phi
and
\tilde{\Phi}
satisfy the properties stated in the lemma. Let

\tilde{\Psi}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\tilde{\Phi}(s)\,ds+\Upsilon(t),\quad t\geq 0.

Then
\Phi=\nabla_{\hat{\Psi}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
and
\tilde{\Phi}=\nabla_{\tilde{\Psi}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
.

We have

\displaystyle\|\Phi-\tilde{\Phi}\|_{t}

\displaystyle=\|\nabla_{{\Psi}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})-\nabla_{\tilde{\Psi}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})\|_{t}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Gamma}\|{\Psi}-\tilde{\Psi}\|_{t}

\displaystyle=C_{\Gamma}\left\{\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\Biggl|\int_{0}^{s}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\left[\Phi(s)-\tilde{\Phi}(s)\right]ds\Biggr|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Gamma}C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|{\Phi}-\tilde{\Phi}\|_{s}ds.

Here, the first inequality follows from the Lipschitz property of the derivative map
\nabla\Gamma
, stated in Proposition
4.5
,
and the third inequality follows from Assumption
4.12
. By Grönwall’s inequality, we must have

\|\Phi-\tilde{\Phi}\|_{t}=0
for all
t\geq 0
, implying that
\Phi=\tilde{\Phi}
.

Next, we establish the existence of
\Phi
by a standard Picard iteration argument.

Let
\Phi^{0}=0
, and recursively define, for
t\geq 0
,

{\Psi}^{k}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\,{\Phi}^{k-1}(s)\,ds+\Upsilon(t),

and let

\Phi^{k}:=\nabla_{{\Psi}^{k}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x}).

Then

\|{\Psi}^{1}\|_{t}=\|\Upsilon\|_{t}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|\upsilon\|_{s}ds\leq C_{\upsilon}t,\quad t\geq 0.

For
k\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\Psi}^{k}(t)-\Psi^{k-1}(t)\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\left(\Phi^{k-1}(s)-\Phi^{k-2}(s)\right)ds\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi^{k-1}(s)-\Phi^{k-2}(s)\|\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi^{k-1}-\Phi^{k-2}\|_{s}\,ds.

Thus, by Proposition
4.5
and Assumption
4.12
, for
k\geq 2
,

\|\Phi^{k}-\Phi^{k-1}\|_{t}\leq C_{\Gamma}\|\Psi^{k}-\Psi^{k-1}\|_{t}\leq C_{\Gamma}C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi^{k-1}-\Phi^{k-2}\|_{s}\,ds.

Noting that
\Phi^{0}\equiv 0
, a standard Picard iteration argument gives

\|\Phi^{k+1}-\Phi^{k}\|_{t}\leq\frac{(C_{\Gamma}C_{\tilde{b}})^{k}}{k!}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi^{1}\|_{s}\,ds\leq\frac{(C_{\Gamma}C_{\tilde{b}})^{k}C_{\upsilon}t^{2}}{2\cdot k!}.

This implies that for any
t\geq 0
,
\{\|\Psi^{k+1}-\Psi^{k}\|_{t}\}_{k}
is a Cauchy sequence.
Thus, there exists
\Psi\in\mathbb{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\Psi^{k}\to\Psi
in
\mathbb{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Let

\Phi:=\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x}).

Then, by Lipschitz continuity of the map
\nabla\Gamma
,

\Phi^{k}\to\Phi\quad\text{uniformly on compact intervals, as }k\to\infty.

Since

\Psi^{k}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\Phi^{k-1}(s)\,ds+\Upsilon(t),

\Phi^{k}\to\Phi
u.o.c. as
k\to\infty
, and
D\tilde{b}
and
\upsilon
are both uniformly bounded,

\Psi
must satisfy

\Psi(t)=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\Phi(s)\,ds+\Upsilon(t),\qquad t\geq 0.

In summary, we have established the uniqueness and existence of the process
\Phi
.

Next, we establish bounds on
\|\Psi\|_{t}
. We have

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{t}

\displaystyle=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left|\int_{0}^{s}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\Phi(r)\,dr+\Upsilon(s)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\upsilon}t+C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi\|_{s}\,ds\leq C_{\upsilon}t+C_{\tilde{b}}C_{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi\|_{s}\,ds.

In the last inequality, we used the uniform boundedness of
D\tilde{b}
, the Lipschitz continuity of the map
\nabla\Gamma
, and the fact that

0=\nabla_{0}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
, where, with a slight abuse of notation,
0
denotes the
d
-dimensional process that takes value
0
in all coordinates, for all times.

By Grönwall’s inequality,

\|{\Psi}\|_{t}<C_{\upsilon}t\cdot\exp\left(C_{\tilde{b}}C_{\Gamma}t\right)\quad\text{for all }t\geq 0.

Finally, we consider
\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\Psi\|_{t}
. For
t\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}(t)-\Psi(t)\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s;\varepsilon\Upsilon))-\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))}{\varepsilon}ds-\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\Phi(s)\,ds\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\left[\frac{\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)+\varepsilon\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))-\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))}{\varepsilon}-D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\Phi(s)\right]ds\right\|.

For any given
s\in[0,t]
, we have

\frac{\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)+\varepsilon\Phi(s))-\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))}{\varepsilon}=\int_{0}^{1}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)+\delta\varepsilon\Phi(s))\Phi(s)d\delta,

so we have

\displaystyle\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}(t)-\Psi(t)\|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\Bigg[\frac{\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)+\varepsilon\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))-\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)+\varepsilon\Phi(s))}{\varepsilon}+

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}\left(D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)+\delta\varepsilon\Phi(s))-D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\right)\Phi(s)d\delta\Bigg]ds\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}C_{\tilde{b}}\|\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)-\Phi(s)\|ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{0}^{1}C_{\tilde{b}}\|\delta\varepsilon\Phi(s)\|^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}\|\Phi(s)\|\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}C_{\tilde{b}}\|\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)-\Phi(s)\|ds+\int_{0}^{t}C_{\tilde{b}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}\|\Phi(s)\|^{1+{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}}ds.

We first derive a bound on the term
\int_{0}^{t}C_{\tilde{b}}\|\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)-\Phi(s)\|ds
.
To bound
\|\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)-\Phi(s)\|
, recall that

\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\nabla^{\varepsilon}_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x}),

So, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)-\Phi(s)\|

\displaystyle=\|\nabla^{\varepsilon}_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla^{\varepsilon}_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla^{\varepsilon}_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|+\|\nabla^{\varepsilon}_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Gamma}\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\Psi\|_{s}+\|\nabla^{\varepsilon}_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|,

where, in the last inequality, we used the Lipschitz property of the map
\Gamma
,
which directly implies the Lipschitz property of the map
\nabla^{\varepsilon}\Gamma
. Thus,

\displaystyle\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}(t)-\Psi(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tilde{b}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi(s)\|^{{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}+1}\,ds+C_{\Gamma}C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\Psi\|_{s}\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tilde{b}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi\|_{s}^{{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}+1}\,ds+C_{\Gamma}C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\Psi\|_{s}\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|\,ds.

Therefore, we also have

\displaystyle\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\Psi\|_{t}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\tilde{b}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi\|_{s}^{{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}+1}\,ds+C_{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|\,ds

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\Gamma}C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\Psi\|_{s}\,ds.

By Grönwall’s inequality, if we can show that for any
t\geq 0
, the term

C_{\tilde{b}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi\|_{s}^{{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}+1}\,ds+C_{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|\,ds

(176)

goes to zero as
\varepsilon\to 0
, then

\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\Psi\|_{t}\to 0.

For the first term in expression (
176
),

C_{\tilde{b}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi\|_{s}^{{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}+1}\,ds,

since we have established that
\|\Psi\|_{t}<C_{\upsilon}t\cdot\exp\left(C_{\tilde{b}}C_{\Gamma}t\right)
for all
t\geq 0
,
by Lipschitz continuity of the map
\nabla\Gamma
and the fact that
0=\nabla_{0}\Gamma
,

\|\Phi\|_{t}<C_{\Gamma}C_{\upsilon}t\cdot\exp\left(C_{\tilde{b}}C_{\Gamma}t\right)\quad\text{for all }t>0,\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi\|_{s}^{{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}+1}ds<\infty.

Thus, as
\varepsilon\to 0
,

C_{\tilde{b}}\varepsilon^{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Phi\|_{s}^{{\alpha_{\tilde{b}}}+1}\,ds\to 0.

For the second term,

C_{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|\,ds,

by the Lipschitz continuity of both the maps
\nabla\Gamma
and
\Gamma
, and the fact that

0=\nabla_{0}\Gamma=\nabla_{0}\Gamma,

we have

\|\nabla^{\varepsilon}_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|\leq 2C_{\Gamma}\|\Psi\|_{t}.

Since
\int_{0}^{t}\|\Psi\|_{s}ds<\infty
, and by definition,

\nabla^{\varepsilon}_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\to\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\ \text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0,\ \text{ for all }s,

by dominated convergence theorem we have

C_{\Gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)-\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(s)\|\,ds\to 0\quad\text{as }\varepsilon\to 0.

In summary, we have established that as
\varepsilon\to 0
, the term in expression (
176
) goes to zero, implying that

\|\Psi^{\varepsilon}-\Psi\|_{t}\to 0
by Grönwall’s inequality. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Proposition
8.2
.

Let
\Psi
and
\Phi
be the same as in Lemma
C.1
, so that

\Psi(t)=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\Phi(s)\,ds+\Upsilon(t),

and
\Phi=\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
. Also recall the definition of
\Psi^{\varepsilon}
in Eq. (
174
).

Because
\Psi^{\varepsilon}\to\Psi
in
\mathbb{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, by Proposition
4.4
, the limit of

\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\nabla_{\Psi^{\varepsilon}}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})

is
\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
, so we must have that
\partial_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}
, the limit of
\partial_{\Upsilon}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, coincides with the process
\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
.
This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

C.2
Proofs of Proposition
8.5

Proof.

By Proposition
4.11
, for all
\psi\in\mathbb{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\nabla_{\psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
exists, lies in
\mathbb{D}_{\ell,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and

\nabla_{\psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})(t+)=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi](t)\quad\text{for all }t>0,

and
\nabla_{\psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
is continuous at times
t>0
when
\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)>0
, or when
\tilde{Z}^{x}_{j}=0
for more than one
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,d\}
.

Thus, by Proposition
8.2
,
\partial_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}
exists, and

\partial_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\nabla_{\Psi}\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
,
where

\Psi(t)=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\partial_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\,ds+\Upsilon(t).

Consequently,
\partial_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(t+)\in D_{\ell,r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and

\partial_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}(t+)=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi](t)\ \text{ for all }t\geq 0.

It remains to prove that there exists a unique derivative process
D_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}
along
\tilde{Z}^{x}
in the direction
\Upsilon
, and part (iii) holds.
We claim that a.s.
D_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi]
.

First, since
\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi]
is the right-continuous regularization of
\partial_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}
, we have

\Psi(t)=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi](s)\,ds+\Upsilon(t).

Next, because by definition
\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi]
solves the derivative problem along
\tilde{Z}^{x}
for
\Psi
, we have

\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi](t)=\Psi(t)+\eta_{\Psi}(t),

with
\eta_{\Psi}(0)\in\text{span}[R(x)]
,
\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi](t)\in H_{\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)}
, and

\eta_{\Psi}(t)-\eta_{\Psi}(s)\in\text{span}\left[\bigcup_{r\in(s,t]}R(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\right]\quad\text{for all }0\leq s<t<\infty.

Because
\Psi(0)=0
,
\eta_{\Psi}(0)\in\text{span}[R(x)]
,

\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi](0)\in H_{x}
, and
H_{x}\cap\text{span}[R(x)]=\{0\}
by Lemma 8.1 of
[
78
]
,
we must have

\eta_{\Psi}(0)=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(0)=0.

Thus,
\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi]
is a derivative process along
\tilde{Z}^{x}
in the direction
\Upsilon
.
By pathwise uniqueness, a.s.
D_{M}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}[\Psi]
.
This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

C.3
Proof of Proposition
8.6

Proof.

Let

\tilde{\Phi}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,t)\upsilon(s)\,ds.

We wish to show that
\tilde{\Phi}
is a derivative process along
\tilde{Z}^{x}
in the direction
\Upsilon
,
so that by pathwise uniqueness we can conclude that a.s.
D_{\Upsilon}\tilde{Z}^{x}=\tilde{\Phi}
.

Let
\eta^{s}(t)
be such that

D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,t)=I+\int_{s}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,r)\,dr+\eta^{s}(t),\qquad 0\leq s<t<\infty.

Then,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,t)\upsilon(s)\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\left[I+\int_{s}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,r)\,dr+\eta^{s}(t)\right]\upsilon(s)\,ds

\displaystyle=\Upsilon(t)+\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\left[\int_{0}^{r}D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,r)\upsilon(s)\,ds\right]dr+\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{s}(t)\upsilon(s)\,ds.

Let

\eta_{\tilde{\Phi}}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{s}(t)\upsilon(s)\,ds.

Then,

\tilde{\Phi}(t)=M(t)+\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\tilde{\Phi}(r)\,dr+\eta_{\tilde{\Phi}}(t).

By the definition of
D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,t)
, for each
0\leq s<t<\infty
,

D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,t)\in H_{\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)}.

Thus,

\tilde{\Phi}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}D{\tilde{Z}^{x}}(s,t)\upsilon(s)\,ds\in H_{\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)}.

We also have
\eta_{\tilde{\Phi}}(0)=0\in\text{span}[R(x)]
and
\tilde{\Phi}(0)=0\in H_{x}
.

For all
0\leq s<t<\infty
,

\displaystyle\eta_{\tilde{\Phi}}(t)-\eta_{\tilde{\Phi}}(s)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{r}(t)\upsilon(r)\,dr-\int_{0}^{s}\eta^{r}(s)\upsilon(r)\,dr

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{s}(\eta^{r}(t)-\eta^{r}(s))\upsilon(r)\,dr+\int_{s}^{t}\eta^{r}(t)\upsilon(r)\,dr.

By definition, for each
r\in[0,s]
,

\eta^{r}(t)-\eta^{r}(s)\in\text{span}\left[\bigcup_{u\in(s,t]}R(\tilde{Z}^{x}(u))\right],

so

\int_{0}^{s}(\eta^{r}(t)-\eta^{r}(s))\upsilon(r)\,dr\in\text{span}\left[\bigcup_{u\in(s,t]}R(\tilde{Z}^{x}(u))\right].

For
r\in(s,t)
,

\eta^{r}(t)\in\text{span}\left[\bigcup_{u\in(r,t]}R(\tilde{Z}^{x}(u))\right]\subset\text{span}\left[\bigcup_{u\in(s,t]}R(\tilde{Z}^{x}(u))\right],

so

\int_{s}^{t}\eta^{r}(t)\upsilon(r)\,dr\in\text{span}\left[\bigcup_{u\in(s,t]}R(\tilde{Z}^{x}(u))\right]

as well. Thus,

\eta_{\tilde{\Phi}}(t)-\eta_{\tilde{\Phi}}(s)\in\text{span}\left[\bigcup_{u\in(s,t]}R(\tilde{Z}^{x}(u))\right].

Therefore,
\tilde{\Phi}
is a derivative process along
\tilde{Z}^{x}
in the direction
\Upsilon
.
By pathwise uniqueness, we conclude the proof of the proposition.
∎

Appendix D
Exact Simulation of the Triple
(
88
)

Recall that the distribution of the random time point
S\in(t,T)
is given by:

\mathbb{P}(S<s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1-2\Phi\left(-\sqrt{2\beta(s-t)}\right)}{1-2\Phi\left(-\sqrt{2\beta(T-t)}\right)},&\beta>0;\\
\frac{\sqrt{s-t}}{\sqrt{T-t}},&\beta=0,\end{array}\right.

(177)

which can be efficiently simulated using standard methods such as inverse transform.
When
\beta>0
, the distribution of
S
is also the same as that of a quadratic function of a truncated normal random variable,
for which efficient simulation packages exist (in e.g., Julia).
Thus, we focus our attention on exact simulation of the triple
\left(\tau_{j},\tilde{Z}^{t,x}_{j}(s),B_{j}(\tau_{j}\wedge s)\right)
in expression (
88
),
where we recall that
\tilde{Z}_{j}^{t,x}
is the reflected Brownian motion defined in Eq. (
86
), and

\tau_{j}:=\inf\{r\geq t:\tilde{Z}_{j}^{t,x}(r)=0\}
is the first hitting time to zero of
\tilde{Z}_{j}^{t,x}
since time
t
.
To simplify notation, we drop the subscript
j
, and suppose that time starts at
t=0
.
Thus, we focus on simulating the triple
\left(\tau,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),B(\tau\wedge s)\right)
,
where
B(\cdot)
is a one-dimensional standard Brownian motion,
\tilde{Z}^{x}
is the associated reflected Brownian motion with initial state
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
drift
\gamma
and variance
\sigma^{2}
, i.e.,
\tilde{Z}^{x}=\Gamma(\tilde{X}^{x})
, where

\tilde{X}^{x}(t)=x+\gamma t+\sigma B(t)

is the free part of
\tilde{Z}^{x}
, and
\Gamma
the one-dimensional Skorokhod map,
and
\tau=\inf\{r\geq 0:\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)=0\}
is the first hitting time of
\tilde{Z}^{x}
(equivalently,
\tilde{X}^{x}
) to zero.
We restrict the drift
\gamma
to be non-positive, to ensure that
\tilde{Z}^{x}
hits
0
with probability
1
.
Our strategy is to first simulate
\tau
, and then simulate

\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)
and
B(t\wedge\tau)
, conditioned on the value of
\tau
.

First, by Section 13.2 of
[
99
]
,
\tau
has an explicit density function
f_{\tau}
given by

f_{\tau}(t)=\frac{x}{s^{3/2}}\phi\left(\frac{x+\gamma t}{\sqrt{t}}\right),

(178)

where
\phi
is the density function of a standard normal distribution, i.e.,

\phi(y)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-y^{2}/2}.

(179)

By a simple rescaling, for general
\sigma>0
, the density function
f_{\tau}

of
\tau
is given by

f_{\tau}(t)=\frac{x}{\sigma t^{3/2}}\phi\left(\frac{x+\gamma t}{\sigma\sqrt{t}}\right).

(180)

This is an inverse Gaussian distribution with parameters
-x/\gamma
and
x^{2}/\sigma^{2}
,
which can be efficiently simulated using standard packages in programming languages such as Julia.

Suppose that we have generated an instance of
\tau
.
We next simulate
\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)
and
B(s\wedge\tau)
, conditioned on
\tau
.
There are two cases to consider,
s\geq\tau
and
s<\tau
.

In the first case
s\geq\tau
, we have

B(s\wedge\tau)=B(\tau)=\sigma^{-1}\left(\tilde{Z}^{x}(\tau)-x-\gamma\tau\right)=-(x+\gamma\tau)/\sigma,

because
\tilde{Z}^{x}(\tau)=0
. To simulate
\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)
, note that by the strong Markov property of
\tilde{Z}^{x}
,
conditioned on
\tilde{Z}^{x}(\tau)=0
,
\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)
has the same distribution as
\tilde{Z}^{0}(s-\tau)
,
where
\tilde{Z}^{0}
is a reflected Brownian motion with initial state
0
, drift
\gamma
,
and variance
\sigma^{2}
.
To simulate marginals of
\tilde{Z}^{0}
, we follow the exact simulation algorithm described in
[
3
]

(also noted in
[
76
,
2
]
).
For completeness, we provide a brief derivation and description of the algorithm here. We have

\tilde{Z}^{0}(t)=W(t)+\sup_{0\leq r\leq t}[W(r)]^{-}=W(t)+\left[-\inf_{0\leq r\leq t}W(r)\right]^{+}.

where
W
is a Brownian motion with drift
\gamma
, variance
\sigma^{2}
, and zero initial state.
In the case
\sigma=1
,
[
3
]
provided the following explicit expression
for the conditional distribution of
-\inf_{0\leq r\leq t}W(r)
,
conditioned on
-W(t)
:

\mathbb{P}\left(-\inf_{0\leq r\leq t}W(r)-y\leq x\mid-W(t)=y\right)=1-e^{-2x(y+x)/t}.

By a simple rescaling, we obtain that for general
\sigma>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(-\inf_{0\leq r\leq t}W(r)-y\leq x\mid-W(t)=y\right)=1-\exp\left(-\frac{2x(y+x)}{\sigma^{2}t}\right).

This suggests an inverse transform method for simulating
-\inf_{0\leq r\leq t}W(r)
, conditioned on the value of
-W(t)
.
From
\left(-W(t),-\inf_{0\leq r\leq t}W(r)\right)
we can obtain
\tilde{Z}^{0}(t)
using the formula above.
We now describe the algorithm.

Step 1. Generate a normal random variable
\tilde{W}
with mean
-\gamma t

and variance
\sigma^{2}t
. This has the same distribution as
-W(t)
.

Step 2. Generate
U
that is uniform on
(0,1)
, and let

\tilde{M}=\frac{\tilde{W}}{2}+\frac{\sqrt{\tilde{W}^{2}-2\sigma^{2}t\log U}}{2}.

Then,
\left(\tilde{M},\tilde{W}\right)
has the same distribution as
\left(-\inf_{0\leq r\leq t}W(r),-W(t)\right)
.

Step 3. Output
\tilde{Z}^{0}(t)=-\tilde{W}+\tilde{M}^{+}
.

Next, we consider the second case, where
s<\tau
.
Because
\tilde{Z}^{x}
remains positive before time
\tau
and
s<\tau
,

\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)=\tilde{X}^{x}(s)
. Furthermore,

B(s\wedge\tau)=B(s)=\sigma^{-1}\left(\tilde{X}^{x}(s)-x-\gamma s\right)
.
Thus, it suffices to simulate the marginal
\tilde{X}^{x}(s)
, given
\tau
and the fact that
s<\tau
.
To this end, note that between time
0
and
\tau
,
\tilde{X}^{x}
is a Brownian meander that starts at
x>0
at time
0
,
remains positive between
0
and
\tau
, and hits
0
at time
\tau
.
It is important to note that Brownian meanders are different from the better known Brownian bridges,
whose paths between end points are not constrained.
Chapter 1 in
[
32
]
provides the following exact method for simulating
\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)
, given
\tau
and
\tau>s
, with appropriate scaling:

Step 1. Generate a standard normal random variable
\tilde{N}
and an independent unit-rate exponential random variable
\tilde{E}
.

Step 2. Output

\sqrt{\left(\frac{x(\tau-s)}{\tau}+\sigma\sqrt{s(\tau-s)/\tau}\tilde{N}\right)^{2}+\frac{2\sigma^{2}s(\tau-s)}{\tau}\cdot\tilde{E}}.

Appendix E
Proofs for Section
5.1

E.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

Proof.

Let
\rho\in\mathbb{R}
, and let
C_{\rho}=\min\{1,e^{\rho T}\}
and
C^{\rho}=\max\{1,e^{\rho T}\}
.
Then, it is not difficult to see that

C_{\rho}\|v\|_{0}\leq\|v\|_{\rho}\leq C^{\rho}\|v\|_{0}.

(181)

Because
\|v\|_{0}<\infty
for all
v\in{\cal B}
, so
\|v\|_{\rho}<\infty
as well.
It is also not difficult to check that
\|\cdot\|_{\rho}
is a well-defined norm.
By Ineq. (
181
), all norms
\|\cdot\|_{\rho}
are equivalent to
\|\cdot\|_{0}
,
so it suffices to show that
(\mathcal{B},\|\cdot\|_{0})
is complete.

Take a Cauchy sequence
\{v^{(n)}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
in
(\mathcal{B},\|\cdot\|_{0})
.
Then

\lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{m,n\geq N}\|v^{(n)}-v^{(m)}\|_{0}=0.

For a fixed
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
,
because
\{v^{(n)}(t,x)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
v^{(n)}(t,x)\to v(t,x)
for some

v(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, as
n\to\infty
.
As the pointwise limit of measurable functions
v^{(n)}
,
v
is a measurable function as well.

Furthermore, for all
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
,
for all
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
N
such that if
n\geq N
, then

\displaystyle\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}\|v(t,x)\|_{\infty}}{w(x)}\leq

\displaystyle~\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}\|v^{(n)}(t,x)\|_{\infty}}{w(x)}+\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|v^{(n)}\|_{0}+\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sup_{n\leq N}\|v^{(n)}\|_{0}+\sup_{m\geq N}\|v^{(m)}-v^{(N)}\|_{0}+\varepsilon<\infty

Thus
\|v\|_{0}<\infty
.

Finally,

\displaystyle\|v-v^{(n)}\|_{0}

\displaystyle=\sup_{(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}}\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}\|v(t,x)-v^{(n)}(t,x)\|_{\infty}}{w(x)}

\displaystyle=\sup_{(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}}\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}\|\lim_{m\to\infty}v^{(m)}(t,x)-v^{(n)}(t,x)\|_{\infty}}{w(x)}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}}\sup_{m\geq n}\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}\|v^{(m)}(t,x)-v^{(n)}(t,x)\|_{\infty}}{w(x)}

\displaystyle=\sup_{m\geq n}\|v^{(m)}-v^{(n)}\|_{0}\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty,

where the convergence follows by the fact that the sequence
\{v^{(n)}\}
is Cauchy.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

E.2
Proof of Lemma
5.2

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{S}=\{(t,s):t\in[0,T),\,s\in(t,T)\}
.
Define the function
\zeta^{(1)}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

\zeta^{(1)}(t,s,x)=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\tilde{\cal H}\left(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\right)\left(\frac{1}{s-t}\int_{t}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)\Bigg],

(182)

and the function
\zeta^{(2)}:[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

\zeta^{(2)}(t,x)=\mathbb{E}\left[\xi(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\left(\frac{1}{T-t}\int_{t}^{T}\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)\right].

(183)

The term
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}d\left[D\tilde{Y}^{t,x}(s)\right]\right]=\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{t,x}(T)
is uniformly bounded over
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
for any fixed
T>0
,
so it suffices to show that
\|\zeta^{(2)}\|_{0}<\infty
, and

\sup_{(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}}\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\int_{t}^{T}\|\zeta^{(1)}(t,s,x)\|_{\infty}ds<\infty.

We first show that
\|\zeta^{(2)}\|_{0}<\infty
.
For
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
,
j=1,2,\cdots,d
,

\displaystyle\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}|\zeta_{j}^{(2)}(t,x)|}{w(x)}

\displaystyle=w(x)^{-1}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\xi(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T-t}}\int_{t}^{T}\langle\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]_{j},dB(r)\rangle\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\right]^{1/2}}{w(x)}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T-t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{t}^{T}\langle\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]_{j},dB(r)\rangle\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\right]^{1/2}}{w(x)}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{T-t}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]_{j}^{2}dr\right]^{1/2}.

Here, the first inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz, and the last equality follows from Itô isometry.

By Proposition
4.16
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\right]\leq C(1+\|x\|^{2\alpha})

for some constant
C=C(T,\alpha,\sigma,C_{\tilde{b}},C_{\Gamma})
, so

\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\right]^{1/2}\leq C^{\prime}(1+\|x\|^{\alpha})

for some constant
C^{\prime}
. But
w(x)=1+\|x\|^{\alpha}
, so

\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\xi^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(T))\right]^{1/2}}{w(x)}\leq C^{\prime}.

By Lemma
4.24
, we also have
\|D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}\|_{T}\leq\tilde{C}

for some constant
\tilde{C}
, so

\frac{1}{(T-t)^{1/2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{t}^{T}\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]_{j}^{2}dr\right)^{1/2}\right]\leq\frac{\tilde{C}^{\prime}(T-t)^{1/2}}{(T-t)^{1/2}}=\tilde{C}^{\prime}.

Thus there is a finite constant that upper bounds

\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}|\zeta_{j}^{(2)}(t,x)|}{w(x)}
uniformly over
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,
j=1,2,\cdots,d
.
Therefore,
\|\zeta^{(2)}\|_{0}<\infty
.

Next, consider
\zeta^{(1)}
.
Recall that

\tilde{\cal H}(x,p)=\inf_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\{(b(x,a)-\tilde{b}(x))^{\top}p+C(x,a)\right\}.

Since
b
is uniformly bounded over all

(x,a)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\times\mathcal{A}
,
\tilde{b}
is uniformly bounded
over
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,
|c(x,a)|\leq\alpha_{2}(1+\|x\|^{\alpha})
uniformly
over
a\in\mathcal{A}
, we have

\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(x,p)\leq\tilde{\alpha}_{1}\|p\|^{2}+\tilde{\alpha}_{2}(1+\|x\|^{2\alpha}),\quad(x,p)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},

(184)

for some constants
\tilde{\alpha}_{1},\tilde{\alpha}_{2}
and
\alpha
. Thus,

\displaystyle\quad\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\int_{t}^{T}|\zeta_{j}^{(1)}(t,s,x)|ds

\displaystyle=\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\int_{t}^{T}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)))\cdot\frac{1}{s-t}\int_{t}^{s}\langle\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]_{j},dB(r)\rangle\right]\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)))\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{s-t}\int_{t}^{s}\langle\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]_{j},dB(r)\rangle\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)))\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{s-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]_{j}^{2}dr\right]^{1/2}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{C}^{\prime}(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\int_{t}^{T}\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\|v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\|^{2}\right]+\tilde{\alpha}_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[1+\|\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)\|^{2\alpha}\right]\right)^{1/2}(s-t)^{-1/2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tilde{C}^{\prime\prime}(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\int_{t}^{T}\left((T-s)^{-1/2}\|v\|_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[w^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\right]^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\left[w^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\right]^{1/2}\right)\cdot(s-t)^{-1/2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}^{\prime\prime\prime}(T-t)^{1/2}\left[\|v\|_{0}\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}ds+\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}ds\right]<\infty.

Here, the first inequality again follows from Cauchy-Schwarz, the second equality follows from Itô isometry,
the second inequality follows from the bound (
184
) on the Hamiltonian
\tilde{\cal H}
,
the third inequality follows from the definition of
\|v\|_{0}
,
the fourth inequality follows by collecting constants and Proposition
4.16
,
from which we deduce that
\mathbb{E}\left[w^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))\right]^{1/2}<\alpha_{3}w(x)
for some constant
\alpha_{3}
,
and the final inequality follows from the fact that the integrals
\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}ds

and
\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}ds
are both finite.

Since our upper bound is uniform over
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
and
j=1,2,\cdots,d
,

\sup_{(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}}\frac{(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}\int_{t}^{T}\|\zeta^{(1)}(t,s,x)\|_{\infty}ds<\infty.

Thus, we can conclude that for
v\in\mathcal{B}
,

\|F(v)\|_{0}<\infty
. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

E.3
Proof of Proposition
5.3

Proof.

Here we only treat the case of zero discount rate, i.e.,
\beta=0
. The more general case follows analogously.
Let
v,\tilde{v}\in(\mathcal{B},\|\cdot\|_{\rho})
. Let
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,d\}
.
Then, for any
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left[F(v)\right]_{j}(t,x)-\left[F(\tilde{v})\right]_{j}(t,x)\right|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\left(\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),\tilde{v}(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)))\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\left.\cdot\left(\frac{1}{s-t}\int_{t}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),\tilde{v}(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdot\left(\frac{1}{s-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{t}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))-\tilde{v}(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[w^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s))\right]^{1/2}e^{-\rho s}\|v-\tilde{v}\|_{\rho}ds

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}w(x)\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}e^{-\rho s}ds\right)\|v-\tilde{v}\|_{\rho},

for some positive constants
C
and
\tilde{C}
.
Here, the first inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz,
and the second inequality follows from the (uniform in
x
) Lipschitz continuity of
\tilde{\cal H}(x,p)
in
p
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\quad w(x)^{-1}e^{\rho t}\left|\left[F(v)\right]_{j}(t,x)-\left[F(\tilde{v})\right]_{j}(t,x)\right|(T-t)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\left(\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\rho(s-t)}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}(T-t)^{1/2}ds\right)\|v-\tilde{v}\|_{\rho}.

The integral

\displaystyle\quad\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\rho(s-t)}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}(T-t)^{1/2}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\rho(s-t)}(s-t)^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}(T-s+s-t)^{1/2}ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\rho(s-t)}\left((s-t)^{-1/2}+(T-s)^{-1/2}\right)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}e^{-\rho(s-t)}ds+\int_{t}^{T}(T-s)^{-1/2}e^{-\rho(s-t)}ds.

By Hölder’s inequality, if
\rho\geq 0
, then

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-1/2}e^{-\rho(s-t)}ds

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{t}^{T}(s-t)^{-3/4}ds\right)^{2/3}\left(\int_{t}^{T}e^{-3\rho(s-t)}ds\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle=\left(4(T-t)^{1/4}\right)^{2/3}\left(-\frac{1}{3\rho}e^{-3\rho(s-t)}\Big|_{t}^{\top}\right)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\leq 4^{2/3}(T-t)^{1/6}\left(\frac{1}{3\rho}\right)^{1/3}.

Similarly,

\int_{t}^{T}(T-s)^{-1/2}e^{-\rho(s-t)}ds\leq 4^{2/3}(T-t)^{1/6}\left(\frac{1}{3\rho}\right)^{1/3}.

Thus, whenever
\rho>4^{5}\tilde{C}^{3}T^{1/2}/3
,

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\rho t}\left|\left[F(v)\right]_{j}(t,x)-\left[F(\tilde{v})\right]_{j}(t,x)\right|(T-t)^{1/2}}{w(x)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|v-\tilde{v}\|_{\rho}

for all
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,
j=1,2,\cdots,J
. This implies that

\|F(v)-F(\tilde{v})\|_{\rho}<\frac{1}{2}\|v-\tilde{v}\|_{\rho},

for sufficiently large
\rho
.
This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

E.4
Proof of Lemma
5.4

Proof.

Let
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{++}^{d}
, and let
\{(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\}_{n}

be a sequence in
[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{++}^{d}
such that

(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to(t,x)\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

We wish to show that

F(v)(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to F(v)(t,x)\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

We first show that if
(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to(t,x)
in such a way that
t^{(n)}\downarrow t
, as
n\to\infty
, then

F(v)(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to F(v)(t,x)
. The case when
t^{(n)}\uparrow t
follows analogously.

Note that

\{(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),\tilde{Y}^{t,x}(s),D\tilde{Y}^{t,x}(s)):s\in[t,T]\}

has the same law as
\{(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s-t),D\tilde{Z}^{x}(s-t),\tilde{Y}^{x}(s-t),D\tilde{Y}^{x}(s-t)):s\in[t,T]\}
.
Thus, we define
(\tilde{Z}^{x},D\tilde{Z}^{x},\tilde{Y}^{x},D\tilde{Y}^{x})
and
\{(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}},D\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}},\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}},D\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}})\}

on the same probability space such that
they are driven by the same Brownian motion.
Consider the terms
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}d\left[D\tilde{Y}^{t,x}(s)\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{x}(T-t)\right]

and
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}d\left[D\tilde{Y}^{t^{(n)},x^{(n)}}(s)\right]\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}(T-t^{(n)})\right]
.
For notational simplicity, write

r^{(n)}=T-t^{(n)}\ \ \text{and}\ \ r=T-t.

(185)

We now proceed to show that as
n\to\infty
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)})\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{x}(r)\right].

By Proposition
4.25
, for each
j=1,2,\cdots,d
,
D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}\to D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}
in the Skorokhod topology.
This implies that there exist strictly increasing bijections
\lambda^{(n)}:[0,T]\to[0,T]
such that as
n\to\infty
,

\left\|D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}-D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}\circ\lambda^{(n)}\right\|_{T}\to 0,\text{ and }\|\lambda^{(n)}-\iota\|_{T}\to 0,

where
\iota:[0,T]\to[0,T]
is the identity map. We have

\displaystyle~\left\|D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)})-D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(r)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)})-D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(\lambda^{(n)}(r^{(n)}))\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(\lambda^{(n)}(r^{(n)}))-D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(r)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\left\|D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}-D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}\circ\lambda^{(n)}\right\|_{T}+\left\|D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(\lambda^{(n)}(r^{(n)}))-D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(r)\right\|_{\infty}.

By Skorokhod convergence, the first term
\left\|D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}-D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}\circ\lambda^{(n)}\right\|_{T}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.
For the second term, note that

\displaystyle~\left|\lambda^{(n)}(r^{(n)})-r\right|\leq\left|\lambda^{(n)}(r^{(n)})-(r^{(n)})\right|+|r^{(n)}-r|\leq\|\lambda^{(n)}-\iota\|_{T}+|t^{(n)}-t|\to 0

as
n\to\infty
, and by Proposition
4.21
part (ii) and Lemma
4.15
, a.s.
r
is a continuity point of
D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}
,
we have that a.s.
\left\|D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(\lambda^{(n)}(r^{(n)}))-D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(r)\right\|_{\infty}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.
Thus, we have shown that a.s. for each
j
,
D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)})\to D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(r)
as
n\to\infty
.
By uniform boundedness of
D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\left[D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)})\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[D_{j}\tilde{Y}^{x}(r\right],\ \text{ so }\ \mathbb{E}\left[\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{t^{(n)},x^{(n)}}(T)\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{t,x}(T)\right].

Next, let
\zeta^{(2)}(t,x)
be as in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, defined in Eq. (
183
).
We now proceed to show that
\zeta^{(2)}(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to\zeta^{(2)}(t,x)

as
n\to\infty
.
To this end, first note that
\{(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)):s\in[t,T]\}

has the same law as
\{(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s-t),D\tilde{Z}^{x}(s-t)):s\in[t,T]\}
.

Thus, for each
j=1,2,\cdots,d
,

\zeta^{(2)}_{j}(t,x)=\mathbb{E}\left[\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(T-t))\left(\frac{1}{T-t}\int_{0}^{T-t}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)\right)\right].

We claim the following.

Claim 1.

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\right)^{2}\right]\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Claim 2.

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{r^{(n)}}\int_{0}^{r^{(n)}}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)-\frac{1}{r}\int_{0}^{r}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)\right)^{2}\right]\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

By Claims 1 and 2, it is immediate that for each
j
,

\zeta^{(2)}_{j}(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to\zeta^{(2)}_{j}(t,x)\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Proof of Claim 1
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)}))\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\right)^{2}\right]\Bigg\}.

By Proposition
4.16
, a.s.

\left\|\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)})-\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)})\right\|\leq\|\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}-\tilde{Z}^{x}\|_{T}\leq C\|x^{(n)}-x\|\to 0\text{ as }n\to\infty,

so by continuity of
\xi
, a.s.

(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)})))^{2}\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Next, define
y=(y_{j})_{j=1}^{J}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
by
setting
y_{j}=\sup_{n}x_{j}^{(n)}
,
j=1,\cdots,d
, and define
a reflecting diffusion
W
by
W=\Gamma(X)
,
where
X(t)=y+C_{\tilde{b}}\bm{1}t+\sigma B(t)
,
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Then, by Theorem 1.1 of
[
72
]
,
a.s.
W(r^{(n)})\geq\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)})
, and

W(r^{(n)})\geq\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)})
for all
n
. Thus, by Assumption
3.5
,

\displaystyle(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)})))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\xi^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)}))+\xi^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)}))\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\alpha_{w}\left[w\left(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)})\right)+w\left(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)})\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\alpha_{w}w\left(W(r^{(n)})\right).\leq 4\alpha_{w}w\left(\|W\|_{T}\right)

By dominated convergence theorem, and the fact that
w\left(\|W\|_{T}\right)
is integrable,

\mathbb{E}\left[(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)})))^{2}\right]\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Next, because
r^{(n)}\to r
, by continuity of the sample paths of
\tilde{Z}^{x}

and
\xi
,

(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)))^{2}\to 0\quad\text{a.s. as }n\to\infty.

A similar argument shows that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\right)^{2}\right]\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Thus,
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r^{(n)}))-\xi(\tilde{Z}^{x}(r))\right)^{2}\right]\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, and we have established Claim 1.

Proof of Claim 2
. Because
r^{(n)}\to r
, and

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{r}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)\right)^{2}\right]<\infty,

it suffices to show that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{r^{(n)}}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)-\int_{0}^{r}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)\right)^{2}\right]\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Let
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,d\}
. We have

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{r^{(n)}}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)-\int_{0}^{r}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{r}\left[\sigma^{-1}\left(D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)\right)\right]^{\top}dB(u)-\int_{r}^{r^{(n)}}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{r}\left[\sigma^{-1}\left(D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)\right)\right]^{\top}dB(u)\right|^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{r}^{r^{(n)}}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)\right]^{\top}dB(u)\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{r}\left\|\left[\sigma^{-1}(D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(u)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u))\right]\right\|_{2}^{2}\,du\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{r}^{r^{(n)}}\left\|\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(u)\right]\right\|_{2}^{2}\,du\right],

where the last equality follows from Itô isometry. By the fact that
\|D\tilde{Z}^{y}\|_{T}
is uniformly bounded
over over
y\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,
t^{(n)}\to t
, and Corollary
4.27
,
both terms in the last expression converge to zero as
n\to\infty
. Thus, we have established Claim 2 as well.

We now establish continuity of the term

\displaystyle\zeta(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\beta(s-t)}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s),v(s,\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(s)))\left(\frac{1}{s-t}\int_{t}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{t,x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T-t}e^{-\beta s}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\left(\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)ds\right]

in
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{++}^{d}
.

For notational simplicity, we consider the case
\beta=0
.
The more general case where
\beta
can be positive can be established using a similar argument.

Let
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,d\}
. For
(t,x),(t^{\prime},x^{\prime})\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{++}^{d}
, with
t^{\prime}>t
,

\displaystyle\zeta_{j}(t,x)-\zeta_{j}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T-t}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\left(\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T-t^{\prime}}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s),v(s+t^{\prime},\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s)))\left(\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq A_{1}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime})+A_{2}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime})+A_{3}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime}),

where

\displaystyle A_{1}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{T-t^{\prime}}^{T-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right|\left|\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right|\right]ds;

(186)

\displaystyle A_{2}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T-t^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s),v(s+t^{\prime},\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s)))\right|\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\left.\left|\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right|\right]ds;

(187)

\displaystyle A_{3}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T-t^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s),v(s+t^{\prime},\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s)))\right|\right.

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\cdot\left|\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}_{j}dB(r)-\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right|\right]ds.

(188)

We now bound the terms
