A_{1},A_{2}
, and
A_{3}
respectively. By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle A_{1}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{T-t^{\prime}}^{T-t}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right|^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}ds.

Using a similar reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, we can show that

\displaystyle A_{1}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq Cw(x)\int_{T-t^{\prime}}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}\left(1+\|v\|_{0}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq Cw(x)\left(\int_{T-t^{\prime}}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}ds+\sqrt{2(T-t)}\|v\|_{0}\int_{T-t^{\prime}}^{T-t}\left(s^{-1/2}+(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\right)\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}w(x)\left(\sqrt{T-t}-\sqrt{T-t^{\prime}}\right)+\tilde{C}w(x)\sqrt{2(T-t)}\|v\|_{0}\left(\sqrt{T-t}-\sqrt{T-t^{\prime}}+\sqrt{t^{\prime}-t}\right)

\displaystyle\to 0\text{ as }t^{\prime}\downarrow t,

for some positive constants
C
and
\tilde{C}
. Here, the second inequality follows from the fact that for
a,b\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}\leq\sqrt{2(a+b)}
.
Thus, if
(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to(t,x)
,
with
t^{(n)}\downarrow t
, then

A_{1}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})\to 0.

Next,

\displaystyle A_{2}(t,t^{\prime},x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T-t^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s),v(s+t^{\prime},\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s)))\right|\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left|\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right|\right]ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T-t}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<T-t^{\prime}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s),v(s+t^{\prime},\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s)))\right|\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\cdot\left|\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right|\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T-t}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<T-t^{\prime}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s),v(s+t^{\prime},\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}ds.

(189)

By Lemma
4.24
, and using Itô isometry,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{s}\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq{\tilde{C}}s^{1/2},

(190)

for some constant
{\tilde{C}}
independent of
s\in(0,T)
and
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,d\}
.

For the term

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s),v(s+t^{\prime},\tilde{Z}^{x^{\prime}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2},

consider
(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to(t,x)
with
t^{(n)}\downarrow t

and
(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})
in place of
(t^{\prime},x^{\prime})
. Then, we upper bound the term

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}(T-t-s)^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

as follows.

\displaystyle\quad\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}(T-t-s)^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))-\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(T-t-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle+2\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\left[(T-t-s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right]^{1/2},

(191)

where, to derive the last inequality, we used the fact that for two random variables
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[(X_{1}+X_{2})^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[X_{1}^{2}\right]^{1/2}+2\mathbb{E}\left[X_{2}^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Next, consider the first term in Ineq. (
191
):

\displaystyle 2\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(T-t-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

We have
s+t^{(n)}\downarrow s+t
for all
s\in(0,T-t)
,
and a.s. for all
s\in(0,T-t)
,

\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)\to\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)\quad\text{ as }\quad n\to\infty.

By the continuity of
\tilde{\cal H}
and that of
v
on
[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{++}^{d}
, and by Lemmas
4.15
and
A.2
,
we have that a.s., for all
s\in(0,T-t)
,

(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\to(T-t-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))).

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(T-t-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[(T-t-s)\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[(T-t^{(n)}-s)\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right]^{1/2}.

Using a similar reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, we can show that

\mathbb{E}\left[(T-t-s)\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right]^{1/2}\leq Cw(x),

and

\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[(T-t^{(n)}-s)\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right]^{1/2}\leq Cw(x),

for some
C>0
that does not depend on
s
or
t
. Thus, the sequence

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(T-t-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

is uniformly integrable, so it converges to zero,
uniformly in
s\in(0,T-t)
.

We then consider the second term in Ineq. (
191
):

\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\left[(T-t-s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right]^{1/2}.

Again using a similar reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, we can show that

\displaystyle\quad\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\left[(T-t-s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\cal H}^{2}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq Cw(x)\left[(T-t-s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\right]\left(1+(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\right).

(192)

Therefore, by Ineqs. (
189
), (
190
), (
191
) and (
192
),

\displaystyle A_{2}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\int_{0}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\Bigg[2\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(T-t-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+2Cw(x)\left[(T-t-s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\right]\left(1+(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\right)\Bigg]ds.

Expanding bracket, and consider the first term

\displaystyle\tilde{C}\int_{0}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}\cdot\left\{2\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<T-t^{(n)}\}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(T-t-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.\quad-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}ds.

We claim that it converges to zero as
(x^{(n)},t^{(n)})\to(x,t)
, with
t^{(n)}\downarrow t
.
This is because the term

\displaystyle\quad\ 2\mathbbm{1}_{\{s\leq T-t^{(n)}\}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(T-t-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s),v(s+t,\tilde{Z}^{x}(s)))\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad-(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{1/2}\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

converges to
0
for all
s\in(0,T-t)
,
is dominated by
8Cw(x)
, with the integrand
s^{-1/2}(T-s)^{-1/2}w(x)
being integrable.
Thus, convergence follows by dominated convergence theorem.

Next, consider the second term (up to a factor of
2\tilde{C}Cw(x)
)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\left[(T-t+s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}+s)^{1/2}\right]\cdot\left(1+(T-s-t^{(n)})^{-1/2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<T-t^{(n)}\}}\right)ds.

We have

\displaystyle\quad\int_{0}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\left[(T-t+s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}+s)^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\left(1+(T-s-t^{(n)})^{-1/2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{s<T-t^{(n)}\}}\right)ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\left[(T-t+s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}+s)^{1/2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T-t^{(n)}}s^{-1/2}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}(T-s-t^{(n)})^{-1/2}\left[(T-t+s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}+s)^{1/2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\left(t^{(n)}-t\right)^{1/2}\int_{0}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T-t^{(n)}}s^{-1/2}\left[(T-s-t^{(n)})^{-1/2}-(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\right]ds,

where, in the last inequality, we used the fact that

(T-t+s)^{1/2}-(T-t^{(n)}+s)^{1/2}\leq\left(t^{(n)}-t\right)^{1/2}.

The integral
\int_{0}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}ds
is finite, so the term

\left(t^{(n)}-t\right)^{1/2}\int_{0}^{T-t}s^{-1/2}(T-t-s)^{-1/2}ds

converges to zero as
n\to\infty
. The integral

\int_{0}^{T-t^{(n)}}s^{-1/2}\left[(T-s-t^{(n)})^{-1/2}-(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\right]ds

is also finite, with the integrand
s^{-1/2}\left[(T-s-t^{(n)})^{-1/2}-(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\right]
converging to zero for each
s\in(0,T-t)
,
so the integral

\int_{0}^{T-t^{(n)}}s^{-1/2}\left[(T-s-t^{(n)})^{-1/2}-(T-t-s)^{-1/2}\right]ds\to 0.

In summary, we have proved that as
(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to(t,x)
with
t^{(n)}\downarrow t
,

A_{2}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})
is upper bounded by the sum of two integrals, both of which converge to zero.
Thus,
A_{2}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})\to 0
as well.

Finally, consider
A_{3}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})
.
We have

\displaystyle A_{3}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T-t^{(n)}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\frac{1}{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}\left(D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r)\right)\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}ds

For
(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})
such that
\|(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})-(t,x)\|_{2}\leq 1
, since
w(x^{(n)})\leq\alpha w(x)
for some constant
\alpha>0
, we can
show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq\alpha\left(1+(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{-1/2}\right)w(x),\quad s\in(0,T-t^{(n)}),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}(D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r))\right]^{\top}dB(r)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
, for all
s\in(0,T-t^{(n)})
, with
\alpha
being redefined appropriately.
Thus, the integrand

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\tilde{\cal H}(\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s),v(s+t^{(n)},\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(s)))\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\cdot\frac{1}{s}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}\left(D\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)-D\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r)\right)\right]^{\top}_{j}dB(r)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}

goes to zero for
s\in(0,T-t^{(n)})
.

In addition,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left[\sigma^{-1}(D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x}(r)-D_{j}\tilde{Z}^{x^{(n)}}(r))\right]dB(r)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\leq\hat{C}s^{-1/2}\quad\text{for some constant }\hat{C},

so by the fact that the integral

\int_{0}^{T-t^{(n)}}\left(1+(T-t^{(n)}-s)^{-1/2}\right)s^{-1/2}ds

is finite, and by dominated convergence theorem, we can show that

A_{3}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})\to 0.

In conclusion,
A_{i}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})\to 0

as
n\to\infty
,
i=1,2,3
, where
(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to(t,x)

such that
t^{(n)}\downarrow t
.
Similarly, we can show that

A_{i}(t,t^{(n)},x,x^{(n)})\to 0
as
(t^{(n)},x^{(n)})\to(t,x)
such that
t^{(n)}\uparrow t
,
i=1,2,3
.

Thus, we have established the continuity
of
\zeta
. Combined with the continuity of
\zeta^{(2)}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{t,x}(T)\right]
,
we have proved that
F(v)
is a continuous
function on
[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{++}^{d}
whenever
v
is
continuous on
[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{++}^{d}
.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of Lemma
9.4

For the proof of Lemma
9.4
, we recall the definitions of
H_{x}
and
R(x)
from Section
4.3
,
and some results from
[
78
]
.

Lemma F.1

(Lemma 8.1 in
[
78
]
)
.

For each
x\in\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,
H_{x}\cap\text{span}[R(x)]=\{0\}
.
For each
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, there exists a unique pair
(v_{y},w_{y})\in H_{x}\times\text{span}[R(x)]
such that

y=v_{y}+w_{y}.

Lemma F.2

(Lemma 8.3 in
[
78
]
)
.

Given
x\in\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,
there exists a unique mapping

{\cal L}_{x}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to H_{x}
that satisfies
{\cal L}_{x}(y)-y\in\text{span}[R(x)]
for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Furthermore,
{\cal L}_{x}
is a linear map.

We can deduce the following result on the derivative process
D\tilde{Z}^{x}
and pathwise derivatives.

Lemma F.3
.

Let
x\in\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
and
y\in\text{span}[R(x)]
. Then a.s. the derivative process
D\tilde{Z}^{x}

evaluated in the direction
y
is zero everywhere, i.e.,

D\tilde{Z}^{x}(t;y)=0\text{ for all }t\geq 0.

As a result, a.s.
\partial_{y}\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)=0
for almost all
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

Since
y\in\text{span}[R(x)]
,
{\cal L}_{x}(y)=0
. Thus, a.s. the derivative process
D\tilde{Z}^{x}
evaluated in the direction
y
satisfies

D\tilde{Z}^{x}(t;y)=\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))D\tilde{Z}^{x}(s;y)ds+\eta_{y}(t),

where the process
\eta_{y}
satisfies the properties of
\eta
described in Definition
4.6
.
Because the zero process solves the derivative problem for the zero process, by Lipschitz continuity of
\Lambda_{\tilde{Z}^{x}}
,

\|D\tilde{Z}^{x}(\cdot;y)\|_{t}\leq C_{\Lambda}C_{\tilde{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\|D\tilde{Z}^{x}(\cdot;y)\|_{s}ds.

By Grönwall’s inequality, we have that a.s.

D\tilde{Z}^{x}(t;y)=0
for all
t\geq 0
. Since
D\tilde{Z}^{x}(\cdot;y)
is the right-continuous regularization of the pathwise derivative
\partial_{y}\tilde{Z}^{x}
,
we also have that a.s.
\partial_{y}\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)=0
for almost all
t\geq 0
. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The following two results are useful consequences of Lemma
F.3
.

Corollary F.4
.

For all measurable
\zeta:\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
with polynomial growth and
t>0
, if
x\in\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,
then for
j\in{\cal I}(x)
,

\left\langle R_{j},D_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[\zeta(\tilde{Z}^{x}(t))\right]\right\rangle=0.

(193)

Proof.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
and
j\in{\cal I}(x)
.
By a density argument that is similar to the proof of Theorem
8.1
, it suffices to establish Eq. (
193
) for
bounded continuously differentiable
\zeta:\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
with bounded gradients.
For such
\zeta
, by chain rule, boundedness of
\zeta
and
D\zeta
, and boundedness of
D\tilde{Z}^{x}
, we have

\left\langle R_{j},D_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[\zeta(\tilde{Z}^{x}(t))\right]\right\rangle=\mathbb{E}\left[D\zeta(\tilde{Z}^{x}(t)D\tilde{Z}^{x}(t;R_{j})\right]=0.

This concludes the proof of the corollary.
∎

Corollary F.5
.

Let
x\in\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
and
j\in{\cal I}(x)
. Then a.s. the derivative process
D\tilde{Y}^{x}

evaluated in the direction
R_{j}
is
-e_{j}
everywhere, i.e.,

D\tilde{Y}^{x}(t;R_{j})=-e_{j}\text{ for all }t\geq 0,

where we recall that
e_{j}
is the
j
th standard unit vector.

Proof.

We have

D\tilde{Z}^{x}(t;R_{j})=R_{j}+\int_{0}^{t}D\tilde{b}(\tilde{Z}^{x}(s))D\tilde{Z}^{x}(s;R_{j})ds+R\left(D\tilde{Y}^{x}(t;R_{j})\right).

By Lemma
F.3
,

R\left(D\tilde{Y}^{x}(t;R_{j})\right)=-R_{j}.

Applying
R^{-1}
to both sides of the equality gives us the desired result.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9.4
.

Recall that
\tilde{V}
is differentiable with
\tilde{v}
being its gradient in
x\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
.
Let
x\in\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
and
j\in{\cal I}(x)
.
From the form of
\tilde{V}
, and Corollary
F.4
, it suffices to prove that

\left\langle R_{j},D_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\beta(s-t)}\kappa^{\top}d\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)\right]\right\rangle=-\kappa_{j}.

By product rule, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\beta(s-t)}\kappa^{\top}d\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\beta(T-t)}\kappa^{\top}\tilde{Y}^{x}(T)-\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)de^{-\beta(s-t)}\right].

Thus,

\displaystyle~\left\langle R_{j},D_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}e^{-\beta(s-t)}\kappa^{\top}d\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\left\langle R_{j},D_{x}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\beta(T-t)}\kappa^{\top}\tilde{Y}^{x}(T)-\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}\tilde{Y}^{x}(s)de^{-\beta(s-t)}\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-\beta(T-t)}\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{x}(T;R_{j})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\kappa^{\top}D\tilde{Y}^{x}(s;R_{j})de^{-\beta(s-t)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~e^{-\beta(T-t)}(-\kappa_{j})-(-\kappa_{j})\left(e^{-\beta(T-t)}-1\right)=-\kappa_{j},

where the second last equality follows from Corollary
F.5
.
This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Appendix G
Proof Sketch of Proposition
9.3

The proof of Proposition
9.3
follows
the same line of reasoning as that of Theorems 3.6 and 3.7 in
[
22
]
.
First, we establish the uniqueness of viscosity solutions for
an appropriately localized version of the HJB equation (
4
) – (
6
),
subject to appropriate boundary conditions (this corresponds to the proof of Theorem 3.6 in
[
22
]
).
Then, we follow the proof of Theorem 3.7 there to
establish uniqueness over the entire domain.

We start with the definition of parabolic jets.

Definition G.1
.

For an upper semi-continuous (USC) function
u:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, define the parabolic superjet of
u
at
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
by

\displaystyle{\cal P}^{+(1,2)}u(t,x):=

\displaystyle~\Big\{(q,p,X)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:u(s,y)\leq u(t,x)+q(s-t)+

\displaystyle~+\langle p,y-x\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle X(y-x),y-x\rangle+o\left(|s-t|+|y-x|^{2}\right),(s,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\Big\}.

Similarly, define the parabolic subjet of
u
at
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
by

\displaystyle{\cal P}^{-(1,2)}u(t,x):=

\displaystyle~\Big\{(q,p,X)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:u(s,y)\geq u(t,x)+q(s-t)+

\displaystyle~+\langle p,y-x\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle X(y-x),y-x\rangle+o\left(|s-t|+|y-x|^{2}\right),(s,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\Big\}.

Define also the closure
c{\cal P}^{\pm(1,2)}u(t,x)
of
{\cal P}^{\pm(1,2)}u(t,x)
to be the set
of all triples
(q,p,X)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that
there are sequences
(t_{n},x_{n})\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\to(t,x)

and
(q_{n},p_{n},X_{n})\in{\cal P}^{\pm(1,2)}u(t_{n},x_{n})
with
(q_{n},p_{n},X_{n})\to(q,p,X)
.

Next, we proceed to sketch the proof of the uniqueness of viscosity solutions for an appropriately localized version of the HJB equation (
4
) – (
6
).
Specifically, let
B_{n}
be the open ball of radius
n
centered at origin,
i.e.,
B_{n}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|x\|_{2}<n\}
. Define
G_{n}:=\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\cap B_{n}
.
Also let
\xi_{n}:\left([0,T]\times\partial B_{n}\right)\cup\left(\{T\}\times G_{n}\right)\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous function so that
\xi_{n}(T,x)=\xi(x)
for all
x\in\partial B_{n}
.
We consider the following parabolic PDE with mixed boundary conditions over the domain
[0,T]\times G_{n}
.

V_{t}(t,x)+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\sigma\sigma^{\top}D_{xx}V(t,x)\right)+{\cal H}\left(x,D_{x}V(t,x)\right)-\beta V(t,x)=0,\quad(t,x)\in[0,T)\times G_{n},

(194)

with boundary conditions

V(t,x)=\xi_{n}(x),\quad\text{ if }(t,x)\in\left([0,T]\times\partial B_{n}\right)\cup\left(\{T\}\times G_{n}\right),

(195)

and

\langle R_{j},D_{x}V(t,x)\rangle=-\kappa_{j},\quad\text{if }x_{j}=0\text{ and }t<T.

(196)

Lemma G.2
.

The parabolic PDE (
194
) – (
196
) has a unique viscosity solution.

The following parabolic analog of Lemma 3.1 in
[
34
]

(also Lemma A.1 in
[
22
]
) is essential for the proof of Lemma
G.2
.
Its proof is an application of the well-known Crandall-Ishii Lemma (Theorem 6.1 in Chapter V of
[
43
]
;
also Theorem 8.3 in
[
27
]
),
using a construction that is similar to that used in the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
33
]
5
5

5

We note a small error in the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
33
]
,
where the terms
2\lambda\langle\bar{x}-\bar{y},x\rangle
(which appeared twice) used
in the constructions of
\tilde{u}
and
\tilde{v}
should be replaced by
2\lambda\langle\bar{x}-\bar{y},x-\bar{x}\rangle

and
2\lambda\langle\bar{x}-\bar{y},x-\bar{y}\rangle
, respectively.
.
We omit details.

Lemma G.3
.

Let
\Omega
be a compact domain in
\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
.
Suppose
u
and
-v
are USC functions such that for every
M>0
there exists a constant
C(M)
such that
for any
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\Omega
,

(q,p,X)\in c{\cal P}^{+(1,2)}u(t,x)\text{ and }\|(t,x,p,X,u(t,x))\|_{2}\leq M\implies q\geq-C(M);

(197)

(q,p,X)\in c{\cal P}^{-(1,2)}v(t,x)\text{ and }\|(t,x,p,X,v(t,x))\|_{2}\leq M\implies q\leq C(M).

(198)

Define
w(t,x,y):=u(t,x)-v(t,y)
. Let
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}>0
,
q\in\mathbb{R}
,
p_{1},p_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\bar{t}\in(0,T)
and
\bar{x},\bar{y}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
satisfy

\left(q,p_{1},p_{2},\alpha_{1}\begin{pmatrix}I&-I\\
-I&I\end{pmatrix}\;+\;\alpha_{2}\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}\right)\in{\cal P}^{+(1,2)}w(\bar{t},\bar{x},\bar{y}).

(199)

Then, there exist symmetric matrices
X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
q_{1},q_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
with
q_{1}-q_{2}=q
, such that

-3\alpha_{1}\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}\;\leq\;\begin{pmatrix}X_{1}-2\alpha_{2}I&0\\
0&X_{2}-2\alpha_{2}I\end{pmatrix}\;\leq\;3\alpha_{1}\begin{pmatrix}I&-I\\
-I&I\end{pmatrix},

and

(u(\bar{t},\bar{x}),q_{1},p_{1},X_{1})\in c{\cal P}^{+(1,2)}u(\bar{t},\bar{x}),\ \text{ and }\ (v(\bar{t},\bar{y}),q_{2},-p_{2},-X_{2})\in c{\cal P}^{-(1,2)}v(\bar{t},\bar{y}).

Proof sketch of Lemma
G.2
.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem 3.6 in
[
22
]
, so we only outline the steps
and highlight main differences. As in
[
22
]
,
let
V_{1}(t,x)
and
V_{2}(t,x)
be viscosity super- and sub-solution of the PDE (
194
) – (
196
), respectively,
with
V_{1}\geq\xi_{n}\geq V_{2}
on
\left([0,T]\times\partial B_{n}\right)\cup\left(\{T\}\times G_{n}\right)
.
Define
V_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t,x):=V_{1}(t,x)+\alpha_{1}g(x)+\alpha_{2}

and
U_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t,x):=V_{2}(t,x)-\alpha_{1}g(x)-\alpha_{2}
for any given
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}>0
.
By way of contradiction, suppose that
V_{2}\nleq V_{1}
on
[0,T]\times G_{n}
.
Then, it can be shown that for some
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}>0
,
U_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}\nleq V_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
on
[0,T]\times G_{n}
,
and, as in Theorem 2.1 of
[
34
]
, that for appropriately modified

\tilde{\Phi}_{*}
and
\tilde{\Phi}^{*}
of
\Phi_{*}
and
\Phi^{*}
defined in Eqs. (
41
) and (
42
) respectively,

\tilde{\Phi}_{*}(t,x,U_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t,x),q,p,X)\leq 0\text{ for }(t,x)\in[0,T)\times G_{n}\text{ and }(q,p,X)\in{\cal P}^{+(1,2)}U_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t,x),

\tilde{\Phi}^{*}(t,x,U_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t,x),q,p,X)\geq 0\text{ for }(t,x)\in[0,T)\times G_{n}\text{ and }(q,p,X)\in{\cal P}^{-(1,2)}V_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t,x).

By standard maximum principle arguments, it can be shown that

\max_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\bar{G}_{n}}\{U_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t,x)-V_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t,x)\}

is achieved at some
(t^{*},x^{*})\in[0,T)\times\left(\partial\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}\cap B_{n}\right)
,
with

\kappa_{0}:=U_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t^{*},x^{*})-V_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}(t^{*},x^{*})>0.

The rest of the proof follows the same steps as in the proof of Theorem 3.6 in
[
22
]
,
until Eq. (A.13) there, where we also write
U_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
and
V_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
as
u
and
v
, respectively, and define instead

\tilde{u}(t,x):=u(t,x)-\frac{\delta}{2}\left(\left\|x-x^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}+(t-t^{*})^{2}\right),

and for
\varepsilon>0
,

\phi(t,x,y):=\tilde{u}(t,x)-v(t,y)-w_{\varepsilon}(x,y).

Let
\left(\bar{t},\bar{x},\bar{y}\right)
a maximum point of
\phi
. Then, similar to the proof of (3.23) in
[
34
]
, as
\varepsilon\to 0
, we have

\frac{\|\bar{x}-\bar{y}\|^{2}_{2}}{\varepsilon}\to 0;\ (\bar{t},\bar{x},\bar{y})\to(t^{*},x^{*},x^{*});\ \tilde{u}(\bar{t},\bar{x})\to u(t^{*},x^{*});\ \tilde{v}(\bar{t},\bar{y})\to v(t^{*},x^{*}).

Define
w(t,x,y):=\tilde{u}(t,x)-v(t,y)
, and use
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
to denote
p(\varepsilon,\bar{x},\bar{y})
and
q(\varepsilon,\bar{x},\bar{y})

in (A.12) of
[
22
]
, respectively (this is to respect the notational convention used in this paper).
Then, from (A.12) of
[
22
]
, we can show that

\left(0,p_{1},p_{2},\ \frac{1}{\varepsilon}\begin{pmatrix}I&-I\\
-I&I\end{pmatrix}\;+\;\frac{\lvert\bar{x}-\bar{y}\rvert^{2}}{\varepsilon}\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}\right)\in{\cal P}^{+(1,2)}w(\bar{t},\bar{x},\bar{y}).

By Lemma
G.3
, we can then find symmetric matrices
X_{1},X_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
q\in\mathbb{R}
such that

-\frac{3}{\varepsilon}\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\
0&I\end{pmatrix}\;\leq\;\begin{pmatrix}X_{1}-\frac{2\lvert\bar{x}-\bar{y}\rvert^{2}}{\varepsilon}I&0\\
0&X_{2}-\frac{2\lvert\bar{x}-\bar{y}\rvert^{2}}{\varepsilon}I\end{pmatrix}\;\leq\;\frac{3}{\varepsilon}\begin{pmatrix}I&-I\\
-I&I\end{pmatrix},

and

(\tilde{u}(\bar{t},\bar{x}),q,p_{1},X_{1})\in c{\cal P}^{+(1,2)}\tilde{u}(\bar{t},\bar{x}),\ \text{ and }\ (v(\bar{t},\bar{y}),q,-p_{2},-X_{2})\in c{\cal P}^{-(1,2)}v(\bar{t},\bar{y}).

The rest of the proof is essentially the same as the correponding parts in the proof of Theorem 3.6 in
[
22
]
. We omit details.
∎

Proof sketch of Proposition
9.3
.

Given Lemma
G.2
,
The rest of the proof of Proposition
9.3
is a standard bootstrapping argument similar to the proof of Theorem 3.7 in
[
22
]
.
Define
\tau_{n}=\tau_{n}(x)
as in the proof of Theorem 3.7 in
[
22
]
.
For any two viscosity solutions
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
of the HJB equation (
4
) – (
6
), for any
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{d}
,
we can obtain the following inequality:

|V_{1}(t,x)-V_{2}(t,x)|\leq\tilde{\alpha}_{w}w(n)\sup_{a(\cdot)}\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{n}(x)<T\right),

for all sufficiently large
n
and some
\tilde{\alpha}_{w}>0
. The fact that
|V_{1}(t,x)-V_{2}(t,x)|=0
can be obtained by letting
n\to\infty
,
and noting that
\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{n}(x)<T\right)
converges to
0
exponentially fast in
n^{2}
, outpacing the polynomial growth of
w(n)
.
∎
